# Cross-AI Plan Review — Phase 38.9

Both lanes ran at **low** reasoning effort and both state that they spot-checked rather than reading every cited line. The orchestrator re-opened the code and plan text for every HIGH finding and every decision-reversal flag before writing the consensus below. Each item is marked **verified** (re-checked on disk) or **lane-only** (not re-checked).

---

## Codex Review

## 1. Summary

**Changes are needed before execution.** The plans provide a coherent foundation-first sequence and preserve many important decisions, but several prescribed mechanisms leave gaps in unsaved-work protection and header coverage. The most consequential issues are nested editors that the proposed guard cannot observe, Cancel controls that bypass it, and asynchronous initialization that can incorrectly mark typed work as clean. This is a targeted source-verified review, not certification of every referenced file or all D-NN coverage. Repository HEAD was `d779d073`; no files were changed. The graph query failed because the sandbox denied its IPC socket.

## 2. Strengths

- **Separate overlay visibility from dismissal.** The existing shell explicitly calls `dismissTop()` on tab retap and hardware Back (`src/navigation/RootNavigator.tsx:110`, `:170`). Plan 01’s callback-free registry appropriately prevents that mechanism from gaining access to destructive dialogs.

- **Preserve dialog layout while changing presentation.** `src/components/ui/ConfirmDialog.tsx:134` delegates actions to `ConfirmActions`, whose measured row/stack layout begins at `:159`. Plan 01 retains this mechanism rather than replacing ADR-174’s behavior with a fixed layout.

- **Account for lazy tab initialization.** `src/navigation/tab-entry.ts:12–24` explains why `initial:false` is necessary, and `:63` supplies it. Plan 07 retains that entry helper and specifically addresses the separate mounted-Profile overwrite case.

- **Recognize backup refresh interference.** `src/screens/BackupSettingsScreen.tsx:96–100` currently reseeds both day fields and clears both errors after a read. Plan 03 correctly identifies that per-field saving must preserve the other draft.

## 3. Concerns

- **HIGH — Plans 02, 10: nested editor state is missing from the guard.**  
  **Evidence:** `src/screens/UpdateContactScreen.tsx:774–782` has a second editor level: choosing a generic custom field opens `CustomFieldFocusedEditor`, whose Cancel returns to the field chooser. `src/components/RelationshipEditor.tsx:52–59` separately owns its draft and picker state. Neither is observable through `session.activeRowKey` alone. Plan 02 also requires RelationshipEditor dirty reporting without listing that component among its modified files.  
  **Required edit:** In `38.9-02-PLAN.md`, Task 3, add explicit dirty/open/close reporting for both nested paths, include `RelationshipEditor.tsx` and its consumers in the ownership list, and specify innermost-first Back behavior. Add behavioral tests proving that Back closes the selected field editor before the outer chooser and protects a typed relationship draft.

- **HIGH — Plans 02, 10: visible Cancel controls bypass the new guard.**  
  **Evidence:** `src/components/MemoryEditor.tsx:246–249` clears the draft directly; its Cancel invokes `close` at `:502`. `src/components/FieldDefForm.tsx:396` invokes `props.onCancel`, wired directly to `setEditor(null)` at `src/screens/CustomFieldsScreen.tsx:334` and `:345`. Update Contact similarly closes its row directly at `src/screens/UpdateContactScreen.tsx:855`. These operations do not remove a route, so `beforeRemove` cannot protect them.  
  **Required edit:** In `38.9-02-PLAN.md`, Tasks 2–3, route user-requested editor cancellation through the same dirty-aware close function used by Back. Keep successful-save closure separate. Test dirty Cancel, clean Cancel, and successful save.

- **HIGH — Plan 02: Add Contact can capture user edits as its clean baseline.**  
  **Evidence:** The editable name exists before initialization completes (`src/screens/CreateContactScreen.tsx:193`, `:442`). Categories arrive asynchronously through `commitCategories` (`:282–289`), while other form data loads independently (`:291–314`). Capturing the *current form* when categories finish can include a name already typed by the user. The prescribed `baseline !== null` condition also leaves that early interval unguarded.  
  **Required edit:** In `38.9-02-PLAN.md`, Task 1, construct the baseline from seed values independently of the live draft. Apply asynchronous seed updates only to the relevant untouched field. Specify failure behavior and test typing before category resolution, failed initialization, and later catalog refresh.

- **MEDIUM — Plans 01, 02, 10, 17: native gesture protection is asserted without a supported mechanism.**  
  **Evidence:** The stacks use `createNativeStackNavigator` (`src/navigation/tabs/DashboardStack.tsx:1`, `:29`), while the existing guard only listens to `beforeRemove` (`src/navigation/discard-keep-guard.ts:22`). The installed navigator explicitly warns that this is not fully supported for native dismissal (`node_modules/@react-navigation/native-stack/src/utils/useDismissedRouteError.tsx:21–23`).  
  **Required edit:** In Plan 01 Task 2 and Plan 02 Task 1, specify how removal prevention integrates with native-stack, using its supported prevention mechanism where applicable. In Plan 17, distinguish Android system-navigation gestures from native stack swipe dismissal; mark unavailable paths as unavailable rather than claiming they passed. Pure decision-function tests do not establish native dismissal protection.

- **MEDIUM — Plans 02, 10: Custom Fields’ editor branch still lacks the shared header.**  
  **Evidence:** `src/screens/CustomFieldsScreen.tsx:324–346` returns `FieldDefForm` directly when editing. That component renders its own heading (`src/components/FieldDefForm.tsx:215–217`) and footer Cancel (`:396`). Replacing only the list branch’s Back does not put the house header on the editor branch. A source test finding one `ShellAppBar` in the file would miss this.  
  **Required edit:** In `38.9-10-PLAN.md`, Task 2, explicitly place the shared header above every Custom Fields render branch and connect its Back to the sub-editor guard. Add a render test with an editor open, not merely a file-level header assertion.

- **MEDIUM — Plan 03: Capture clears a typed new-contact name without checking dirty state. [POSSIBLE DECISION REVERSAL: D-14]**  
  **Evidence:** Capture owns `inlineName` (`src/screens/CaptureScreen.tsx:154–155`). Plan 03 Task 2 prescribes closing the inline surface and clearing that name whenever `inlineOpen` is true. D-14 requires prompting when closing an inner editor would lose typed text.  
  **Required edit:** In `38.9-03-PLAN.md`, Task 2, include the inline draft in the Back resolver and prompt before clearing a meaningful name. Test both untouched and typed inline forms. If unconditional discard is intended, it requires an explicit owner ruling.

- **MEDIUM — Plan 03: pending-note dirty tracking needs lifecycle and failure rules.**  
  **Evidence:** `AssistConfirmation` retains local note state (`src/components/AssistConfirmation.tsx:27–28`). Its parent’s action handlers can finish after a failed write while keeping the item pending (`src/components/PendingConfirmationsSheet.tsx:112–128`, `:142–149`). “Remove a uid when its item resolves” is ambiguous: the handler resolving is not proof that the item disappeared or the note was saved.  
  **Required edit:** In `38.9-03-PLAN.md`, Task 1, prune dirty IDs against actual queue membership, preserve dirty state after failed commits, and reset tracking when the sheet’s drafts are actually discarded. Test failure, external queue removal, close/reopen, and successful confirmation.

- **MEDIUM — Plans 01, 05, 06, 09–16: reading instructions contradict the repository review rule.**  
  **Evidence:** Plan 01 Task 3 says to read “only the import line and the Modal element”; Plan 06 Task 2 says to read listed lines. These are explicit alternatives to the full-file/subsystem reading required by `CLAUDE.md` under “Review the code, not the diff.” The missed nested state above demonstrates the practical consequence.  
  **Required edit:** Replace those narrow `read_first` instructions with full-file reading and named shared-state consumers. For label-only DAO edits, explicitly verify the owning query/transaction remains unchanged rather than relying solely on the supplied line reference.

- **MEDIUM — Plan 01: the new `dismissable` override can weaken declarative destructive confirms. [POSSIBLE DECISION REVERSAL: D-06 / ADR-086]**  
  **Evidence:** Today `src/components/ui/ConfirmDialog.tsx:94` unconditionally makes destructive dialogs non-dismissable. The proposed prop defaults to `!destructive` but does not expressly prohibit `destructive:true, dismissable:true`. The imperative store’s protection would not protect the nine declarative consumers.  
  **Required edit:** In `38.9-01-PLAN.md`, Task 2, define effective dismissal as false whenever destructive is true, regardless of the override. Add a component-level test for the conflicting-props case.

## 4. Suggestions

- Add rendered interaction tests for guard wiring, header presence in alternate branches, and dirty Cancel behavior. The proposed pure comparators and source scans cannot detect those failures.
- Give backup blur saves a per-field request/version rule so a completed refresh cannot overwrite newer typing in that same field (`src/screens/BackupSettingsScreen.tsx:91–105`).
- Before execution, reconcile the `110000–150000` token estimates with the actual executor context budget. File count alone does not justify splitting, but the plans currently provide no demonstrated basis for meeting the required ~50% budget. Any split of these already-seen plans requires owner approval.

## 5. Risk Assessment

**HIGH until the guard and render-branch gaps are corrected.** The architecture is broadly sound, but the plans currently promise stronger protection than their prescribed wiring delivers. Several failures would pass the planned helper/source-contract tests and surface only during the final device pass. Correcting these within the existing owning plans would substantially reduce risk without changing the phase’s product decisions.


---

## Claude Review

# Cross-AI review: Phase 38.9 plans 01–17

I checked the plan claims against the repo with a few greps and a read of `discard-keep-guard.ts` and `universal-fab-logic.ts`. I did not open every cited line. I did not open `38.9-RESEARCH.md`. Anything that rests only on the plan text is marked as unverified.

## 1. Summary

The plans are well sequenced. Foundations come first (01 overlays and dialogs, 07 shell, 08 header), then mechanical sweeps (05, 06, 09–11, 14–15), then docs and the device plan. Every D-NN in scope maps to at least one plan, and most plans pin their behaviour with a source or unit contract. The greps match the plans' inventories:
- The 38 files with real `Alert` use are all covered by plans 01, 02, 04, 05 and 06. The exceptions are the intended `HomeScreen` snooze and two comment-only mentions (`theme-types.ts` and `field-widgets/types.ts`).
- There are 19 raw-`Modal` files plus `overlay-base.tsx`.
- There are 30 real `<Switch` sites. The extra `OrreryV2Canvas.tsx` hits are `switchFx`/`switchRoles` identifiers, not Switch components.
- The 6 `@react-native-picker/picker` files match.

I found no decision reversals. The remaining risks are:
- timing and edge cases in the guard and Back-handling logic;
- a few verification steps that would not catch the failure they claim to;
- a few constraints that cite no source;
- one copy rewrite that touches a decided vocabulary.

## 2. Strengths

- **Plan 01:** the overlay registry deliberately has no close callbacks, with a key-inspection test that enforces it. `Snackbar` keeps the ADR-173 rules (`collapsable={false}`, one `selectFabDialOpen` read before any early return). Only the top overlay's snackbar host renders, and the pure resolvers `resolveSnackbarHostVisible` and `snackbarBottomPadding` are unit-tested.
- **Plan 06:** it spots the one confirm whose Cancel has a side effect (`BackupScreen`'s "Export readable JSON") and sets `dismissable: false`. It also keeps the field-with-values case in `CustomFieldsScreen` non-destructive (Quarantine) while making the empty-field Delete destructive.
- **Plan 06:** the `no-native-alert-contract` test covers static, aliased and dynamic-import forms. The plan includes a self-test for the dynamic-import case, which matters because `widget-linking.ts` uses `import("react-native")`.
- **Plan 07:** the D-54 launch-tab fallback is built as an in-place reset, not a `navigate`. The plan's rationale is that a plain navigate would append history and make Back loop. The plan drives the real `TabRouter` in tests, and a RG-021 guard keeps cross-tab navigation inside `tab-entry.ts`.
- **Plan 07:** the D-50 trap is handled. A nested `navigate("Profile")` would rewrite an existing Profile's params, so the plan pushes onto a mounted Dashboard stack instead.
- **Plans 02 and 04:** the bypass ordering is called out explicitly. It is set after the awaited duplicate-name confirm and reset on write failure. The `findNodeHandle` bug is also pinned by a source assertion.
- **Plan 08:** the single-Back contract is extended from the Settings stack to all five stacks. It uses an explicit pending list that plans 09 and 10 must empty, and pending screens must still have their old Back so the list cannot rot.
- **Plan 13:** the unparseable custom-field date keeps its raw value, which respects the custom-field invariants. The display formatter is fenced off from `src/db/`.
- **Plan 17:** the device plan keeps the runbook as the source of the phone choice, and it forbids real AI calls and Drive uploads without clearance.

## 3. Concerns

**HIGH**

- **[Plan 03 Task 3, D-34] Backup schedule can lose a valid typed value on header Back.**
  - Evidence: the plan commits on `onBlur`/`onSubmitEditing` only. On Android, tapping the header Back or system Back does not reliably fire `blur` before the route unmounts.
  - Failure: the user types "14", taps Back, and sees no save and no prompt. D-34 says "saves when the field is left".
  - Fix: in 03 Task 3, also flush any valid, changed field from a `beforeRemove` or unmount effect (via a ref to the current text). Add a unit test for the flush decision, using a pure `shouldFlushOnLeave` helper.

**MEDIUM**

- **[Plan 01 Task 2 / Plan 02 Task 1] Duplicate discard prompts are not prevented.**
  - Evidence: `useDiscardKeepGuard` (`src/navigation/discard-keep-guard.ts:22-38`) calls `preventDefault` and opens a prompt on every `beforeRemove`. The new `confirmDiscard()` enqueues onto a FIFO queue.
  - Failure: a double-tapped Back, or a gesture plus a header tap, queues two "Discard changes?" prompts. After the user taps Discard on the first, the second appears on a screen that is already gone.
  - Fix: in Plan 01 Task 2, add a per-guard `promptOpenRef`. Skip enqueueing while a prompt is pending, and add a test for it. Add the same dedupe to Plan 03's Capture and sheet-close paths.

- **[Plan 01] The Back-safety claim is verified only negatively.**
  - Evidence: D-24 and the CONTEXT verification say `dismissTop()` "can never close a non-dismissable destructive confirm". The only test is that the registry has no close keys. `RootNavigator.tsx:164-188` still runs `dismissTop()` on hardware Back.
  - Failure: nothing tests the actual Back path with a destructive `DialogHost` confirm open on top of a registered shell-transient item such as the contact picker. This relies on the Android Modal consuming Back first, which is unverified.
  - Fix: in Plan 01 Task 2, have the root back listener early-return `true` (swallow) when `selectAnyOverlayOpen` is true. Alternatively, add a device check for it in Plan 17.

- **[Plan 16 Task 3] The "no other 39 file edited" check passes vacuously.**
  - Evidence: the verify uses `git diff --name-only HEAD -- .planning/phases/39-onboarding`, which shows only uncommitted changes.
  - Failure: an executor who commits per CLAUDE.md sees an empty diff, so the check always passes.
  - Fix: compare against the phase base, for example `git diff --name-only <base>..HEAD`, or assert via `git log` that only `39-AMENDMENTS-PENDING.md` changed.

- **[Plan 15, D-41] "override(s)" rewrites touch an existing vocabulary.**
  - Evidence: Plan 15 renames "Reset overrides?", the System "Overrides" badge, "Participant overrides", "Contact override: …" and "Category override" to "manual changes", "Edited" and so on.
  - Concern: D-41 names "overridden" as developer wording, but "override" is the established Systems and Group Event inheritance vocabulary (the milestone-2 dossiers and ADR-077/078). The plans list no ADR or dossier check.
  - **[OWNER?] [POSSIBLE DECISION REVERSAL]** against the Systems/Group Event dossier terms. Fix: in Plan 15, add a task step grepping `docs/dossier` and `docs/decisions` for "override" as a canonical term. Restrict the rewrite to strings D-41 names, and leave "Reset overrides?" and the System badge until the owner confirms.

- **[Plan 17 Task 1] Dependency removal has no source and sits in the device plan.**
  - Evidence: the plan says the removal is a RESEARCH suggestion, so no D-NN or ADR cites it. Removing a native dependency changes autolinking.
  - Fix: label it "this plan only" and move the `npm uninstall` into Plan 12 Task 3, so the unit gate sees it. If it must stay, cite D-28.
  - Related: `package.json` and the lockfile are modified in the plan that is meant to be device-only.

- **[Plan 14 Task 1] The glossary contract is fragile as described.**
  - Evidence: it scans string literals and JSX text for "Dashboard", "dashboard" and "favourite" across all of `src`, with allowlists for identifiers, test IDs and route names.
  - Failure: false positives on comments, log scopes and identifiers; false negatives on strings built dynamically, such as the `POPULATION_LABELS` interpolation at `HomeScreen.tsx:1708`.
  - Fix: use a TS-AST walk of string-literal and JSX-text nodes, as `accent-foreground-roles.ts` does, rather than a regex. Alternatively scope it to a maintained list of user-facing files.

**LOW**

- **[Plan 11] Disabled switch a11y mismatch.** `resolveSwitchVisual` returns `on: false` when disabled, and `accessibilityState.checked` is driven by `on`. TalkBack announces Off while the stored value is On. D-26 says "shows Off", which is visual. Keep `checked` bound to the real value, or confirm the announcement is intended.

- **[Plan 11] Off-track contrast.** The plan states that the `borderStrong` outline is 1.6–2.1:1, below the 3:1 non-text guideline, and tests only the thumb. This is the owner's look (D-47). Record it explicitly in the Plan 17 owner checklist and in Phase 40's backlog.

- **[Plan 07] `FAB_ROUTES` is not derived from the FAB actions.** `universal-fab-logic.ts` hardcodes `tab: "DashboardTab"` at lines 132, 190, 195 and 203, which the plan changes. The `FAB_ROUTES` list itself is hand-written. Add a test that every `FabNavigateIntent["screen"]` member is in `FAB_ROUTES`.

- **[Plan 01 Task 1] Snackbar position during overlay close is untested.** Registration is effect-driven, and the in-window host unmounts as the Modal fades. A snackbar fired just as a sheet closes could flick between hosts. This is acceptable, but add it as a Plan 17 observation.

- **[Plan 13] The contract cannot detect key misuse.** The `display-date-contract` source scan checks `src/db/` imports and `toISOString().split`, but it cannot see `formatDisplayDate` output reaching a key built elsewhere. A weak guard, so add one unit case where `digest-composition` keeps a separate `formatLocalDate` key field.

## 4. Suggestions

- **Plan 01:** say in the Plan 01 text that `RegisteredModal` must not render its own Snackbar host when it wraps `BaseOverlay`. Task 1 handles this with `renderSnackbarHost`, but the final shape should be recorded in the summary.
- **Plan 03:** add the Capture-sheet and pending-note dedupe tests from the duplicate-prompt concern above.
- **Plan 08:** replace the pending list with a data structure that records the owning plan. A failure message such as "owned by 38.9-10" makes drift diagnosable.
- **Plan 17:** add the following device items. They are already flagged in the plan or are natural follow-ons:
  - two quick Back presses on a guarded form;
  - a snackbar fired while the keyboard is open inside a sheet;
  - a typed Backup-schedule value followed by Back.
- Add a `tsc --noEmit` step to each Task 1 tracer verify. Most already include it, but Plan 17 Task 2 relies on the wave gate alone.

## 5. Risk Assessment

**MEDIUM.** The architecture is sound and the contracts are strong. The risk is concentrated in:
- timing-sensitive Android behaviour (blur on unmount, the double-Back prompt, whether Modal consumes Back before the root listener runs);
- the sweep width of plans 05, 06, 09 and 10, where a single missed mock or contract edit breaks the wave gate;
- the glossary and plain-word rewrites, one of which may collide with decided Systems and Group Event vocabulary.

The HIGH item is a small, local fix. Everything else is fixable in the PLAN.md text before execution, and none of it needs a decision reversal.


---

## Consensus Summary

The two lanes found almost entirely **different** problems, and neither lane disputed the other's findings. Both agree that the architecture and wave order are sound, and that the risk sits in the timing and wiring of the unsaved-work guard, not in the overall design. Neither found an unsourced constraint severe enough to block. Claude counted the sweep inventories (Alert, Modal, Switch, Picker) by grep and found that they match the plans.

### Agreed Strengths
- The overlay registry has no close callbacks, so `dismissTop()` cannot reach a destructive confirm. The existing shell path is at `RootNavigator.tsx:164-188` (both lanes).
- `ConfirmDialog` keeps the measured `ConfirmActions` layout from ADR-174 (both lanes).
- Plan 07 reasons carefully about lazy tab entry and the mounted-Profile overwrite (D-50) (both lanes).
- Plan 03 knows that a backup refresh currently reseeds both fields (Pitfall 8) (codex). Claude's HIGH below shows that the save trigger is still incomplete.

### Agreed Concerns
- **The guard wiring promises more than the planned tests prove.** Codex points to nested editors, Cancel paths and the baseline race. Claude points to blur-on-Back, duplicate prompts and Back-safety being tested only negatively. Both recommend rendered or behavioural tests rather than pure-helper and source-scan tests alone, plus extra device checks in Plan 17.

### Divergent Views
- **Overall risk:** codex rates it HIGH, claude rates it MEDIUM. The orchestrator's re-check agrees that the three HIGHs below are real.
- **Plan size:** codex questions the 110k–150k token estimates against the ~50% executor budget, but asks for no split. Under CLAUDE.md Plan sizing this is not a finding unless a plan is genuinely too large. Splitting any of these plans needs the owner's approval, because the owner has seen them. No action.
- **Plan 15 "override" rewrite:** claude flagged it as a possible reversal and cited ADR-077/078. **That citation is wrong.** ADR-077 is the orrery camera and ADR-078 is Off Limits/AI context. The real evidence is the milestone-2 LOCKED decision D-09-088 ("edit/reset overrides", `docs/dossier/milestone-2/audit/LEDGER.md:1123`) and the Phase 39 discovery workpaper's "Systems Reset Overrides" (`docs/dossier/workpapers/phase-39-onboarding-journey-discovery.md:3949`). D-41's owner answer lists "overridden" as an example of developer wording. It does not name the Systems "Overrides" feature label.

## Cycle 1 — HIGH Concerns (history)

**H1 — Update Contact's nested editors are invisible to the sub-editor guard.** Raised by codex; verified. Plan **38.9-02**, Task 3.
- **Evidence:** the custom-field row editor has two levels. `UpdateContactScreen.tsx:774-783` keeps a local `selected` state, and `CustomFieldFocusedEditor` has `onCancel={() => setSelected(null)}`, which returns to the chooser.
- **Gap:** the guard's `subEditor.open` is only `session.activeRowKey != null`, and `close` is `cancelRow`. Back from the inner editor therefore closes the whole row, not the innermost level as D-14 requires. Dirty from the inner editor also has to be plumbed through `CustomFieldsRowEditor`.
- **Gap:** Task 3 relies on "KeyPeople via RelationshipEditor's draft", but `src/components/RelationshipEditor.tsx` is not in `files_modified` or in Task 3's `<files>` (`38.9-02-PLAN.md:7-29`, `:172`).
- **PLAN.md change** (`38.9-02-PLAN.md` Task 3):
  - Add `RelationshipEditor.tsx` to `files_modified` and to Task 3's `<files>`.
  - Specify `onDirtyChange` plumbing for RelationshipEditor and for CustomFieldsRowEditor → CustomFieldFocusedEditor.
  - Specify innermost-first close: Back with `selected` set returns to the field chooser (prompting if dirty) before `cancelRow`.
  - Add `<behavior>` cases: inner editor Back goes to the chooser; a dirty relationship draft prompts.

**H2 — Add Contact's clean baseline can capture user typing, and failure can leave the guard permanently off.** Raised by codex; verified. Plan **38.9-02**, Task 1.
- **Evidence:** the plan captures `baseline` from the live form "once the initial category resolution has finished" and guards only `baseline !== null && …` (`38.9-02-PLAN.md:122`). The name input is editable from first render (`CreateContactScreen.tsx:193`, `:442`), and categories arrive asynchronously through `commitCategories` (`:282-289`).
- **Failure 1:** a name typed before resolution becomes part of the "clean" seed, so Back silently discards it.
- **Failure 2:** if category resolution never commits (load error path at `:305-308`), the baseline stays null and E-01, this phase's headline guard, is never active.
- **PLAN.md change** (`38.9-02-PLAN.md` Task 1):
  - Build the seed signature from the seed values (empty name, the resolved default category, default interval, and so on), not from the live `formState`. Alternatively, treat any non-seed user edit before the baseline exists as dirty.
  - Define the failed-initialization behaviour: guard on with the pre-resolution seed.
  - Add `<behavior>` cases: typing before category resolution is dirty; a failed category load still guards; a later catalog refresh does not mark the form dirty.

**H3 — Backup schedule loses a valid typed value when the user leaves with Back.** Raised by claude; verified against the plan text. Plan **38.9-03**, Task 3.
- **Evidence:** the save is wired only to `onBlur` and `onSubmitEditing` (`38.9-03-PLAN.md:146`, acceptance `:152`). On Android, header Back or system Back does not reliably blur a focused TextInput before the screen unmounts. D-34 says "a valid typed count saves when the field is left", and leaving the screen is leaving the field.
- **PLAN.md change** (`38.9-03-PLAN.md` Task 3):
  - Add a leave-flush: on `beforeRemove` or unmount, commit any valid, changed field from a ref to the current text.
  - Add a pure `shouldFlushOnLeave(seed, text)` helper with tests.
  - Add a per-field request/version rule so that a completed refresh cannot overwrite newer typing in the same field (codex suggestion, `BackupSettingsScreen.tsx:91-105`).
  - Add "type a valid count, press Back, reopen" to Plan 17's device list.

## Cycle 1 — Actionable Non-HIGH Concerns (history)

### MEDIUM

- **M1 [OWNER?] — Visible Cancel buttons on in-screen editors bypass the guard.** Raised by codex; partly verified. Plans **02** and **10**.
  - Evidence: Update Contact's `onCancel = cancelRow` (`UpdateContactScreen.tsx:855`); FieldDefForm Cancel calls `setEditor(null)` (per `38.9-02-PLAN.md:98`); MemoryEditor Cancel calls `close` (codex cites `MemoryEditor.tsx:246-249`, `:502`, lane-only).
  - Why it is the owner's: D-14 covers Back (header, system, gesture). Route forms already prompt on Cancel because Cancel calls goBack and fires beforeRemove. Whether an explicit in-editor Cancel should also prompt is a product call.
  - PLAN.md change, if the owner says prompt: in `38.9-02-PLAN.md` Tasks 2–3, route user Cancel through the same dirty-aware close used by Back, keep save-close separate, and test dirty Cancel, clean Cancel and save.
  - If the owner says no prompt: add an explicit deferral or rejection line to Plan 02.

- **M2 [OWNER?] [POSSIBLE DECISION REVERSAL — D-14 vs D-33 wording] — Capture's Back clears a typed inline new-contact name without prompting.** Raised by codex; verified. Plan **03**, Task 2.
  - Evidence: the plan sends close-inline to `setInlineOpen(false)` and clears `inlineName` (`38.9-03-PLAN.md:112`, `:120`).
  - D-14 says to prompt if typed text would be lost. D-33 says only that Back "first closes an open New-contact sheet", and prompts only for the note.
  - PLAN.md change: in `38.9-03-PLAN.md` Task 2, add a `inlineName` input to `resolveCaptureBack`, giving prompt-discard-inline when the name is non-empty, and add tests. The other option is an owner ruling that recording an unconditional close is intended.

- **M3 [POSSIBLE DECISION REVERSAL — D-06 / ADR-086] — The new `dismissable` prop lets a declarative destructive confirm become dismissable.** Raised by codex; verified. Plan **01**, Task 2.
  - Evidence: today the code is `dismissable={!destructive}` with no override (`ConfirmDialog.tsx:94`). The plan's rules are "effective dismissable defaults to !destructive; explicit dismissable:false wins" (`38.9-01-PLAN.md:196`, `:203`). Nothing forbids `destructive && dismissable:true`, and the store-level no-op (`:262`) does not cover the 9 declarative consumers.
  - PLAN.md change (`38.9-01-PLAN.md` Task 2): set effective dismissable to `!destructive && (dismissable ?? true)`, apply it in both ConfirmDialog and confirm-store, and add a test for the conflicting props.

- **M4 — The Custom Fields editor branch gets no shared header.** Raised by codex; verified against the plan table. Plan **10**, Task 2.

- **M5 — Native-stack swipe dismissal is not protected by `beforeRemove` alone.** Raised by codex; lane-only. Plans **01**, **02** and **17**.
  - Evidence: codex cites `node_modules/@react-navigation/native-stack/src/utils/useDismissedRouteError.tsx:21-23` and `discard-keep-guard.ts:22`.
  - PLAN.md change:
    - In Plan 02 Task 1, state how native-stack dismissal is prevented (for example the `gestureEnabled` / `usePreventRemove` route option on guarded screens), or state that Android has no swipe-dismiss on these stacks.
    - In Plan 17, list Android system-gesture Back separately from stack swipe-dismiss, and mark unavailable paths as N/A.

- **M6 — Pending-note dirty tracking has no rule for failure or lifecycle.** Raised by codex; partly verified. Plan **03**, Task 1.
  - Evidence: "Remove a uid from the set when its item resolves" (`38.9-03-PLAN.md:93`). Codex cites failed writes keeping the item pending (`PendingConfirmationsSheet.tsx:112-128`, `:142-149`), lane-only.
  - PLAN.md change: prune dirty uids against actual queue membership, keep dirty after a failed commit, reset on a confirmed discard or sheet close, and add those tests.

- **M7 — Narrow `read_first` instructions contradict CLAUDE.md "Review the code, not the diff".** Raised by codex; verified.

- **M9 [OWNER?] [POSSIBLE DECISION REVERSAL — milestone-2 D-09-088 term] — Plan 15 renames the Systems "Overrides" feature wording.** Raised by claude (wrong ADR cited); evidence corrected by the orchestrator. Plan **15**.

# Cycle 2 — Cross-AI Plan Review (after fix commit 867311fc)

**Lane health (cycle 2):** both lanes ran; neither dropped. SELF_CLI=none (owner-authorized). Prompt: 399,996 bytes. It inlined CONTEXT (with D-61/D-62), LEDGER, the cycle-1 findings and all 17 current plans; RESEARCH, PATTERNS and VALIDATION were referenced by path.
- codex: `gpt-6.1-sol (reasoning=low)`, 103 s, 9,260 bytes, ok.
- claude: `sonnet (reasoning=low)`, 164 s, 11,868 bytes, ok.

The orchestrator re-opened the plans and the code for every finding counted below. Each item is marked **verified** or **dropped/corrected**.

## Cycle 2 — Codex Review

### Summary

Cycle 2 is **not yet converged**. Most cycle-1 fixes are explicit and consistent, including the nested Update Contact editors, destructive-dialog dismissal, and the owner’s D-61/D-62 rulings. Remaining issues are the backup save/version race, Memory’s innermost-first Back behavior, incomplete prompt deduplication, and device coverage for Cancel. No files were modified. The findings below concern planned behavior, not implemented fixes.

### Cycle-1 resolution table

Plan references below are under `.planning/phases/38.9-ux-standards/`.

| Finding | Status | Evidence and mechanism |
|---|---|---|
| H1 | RESOLVED | `38.9-02-PLAN.md:211–215` lifts custom-field selection, exposes RelationshipEditor draft state/close, and specifies innermost-first closure. This addresses the private selection at `src/screens/UpdateContactScreen.tsx:774–783`. |
| H2 | RESOLVED | `38.9-02-PLAN.md:128–139` builds the baseline from seed values and enables guarding from first render, including failed initialization. |
| H3 | PARTIAL | `38.9-03-PLAN.md:150–156` adds leave-flush and version tracking, but advances the committed version before write success. See concern N1. |
| M1 | RESOLVED | D-61 at `38.9-CONTEXT.md:151`; `38.9-02-PLAN.md:173,213–215` routes editor Cancel through `requestClose`, while successful saves close directly. |
| M2 | RESOLVED | `38.9-03-PLAN.md:117,125` distinguishes empty inline-name closure from typed-name discard confirmation under D-14. |
| M3 | RESOLVED | `38.9-01-PLAN.md:197,204–205` requires `!destructive && (dismissable ?? true)` in both store and declarative dialog. |
| M4 | RESOLVED | `38.9-10-PLAN.md:86,135` puts the header above every Custom Fields branch and adds editor-open render tests. |
| M5 | RESOLVED | `38.9-02-PLAN.md:108,136` records Android’s mechanism; installed native-stack sets `nativeBackButtonDismissalEnabled={false}` at `NativeStackView.native.tsx:411`. Predictive Back is disabled at `app.config.ts:76`. Plan 17 separates system gesture from unavailable stack swipe-dismiss (`:89`). |
| M6 | RESOLVED | `38.9-03-PLAN.md:93–98` prunes against actual queue membership, preserves dirty state after failed commits, and resets on actual closure. |
| M7 | PARTIAL | Full-file instructions were added at Plan 01 `:230`, Plans 05/06, and Plans 13–16. Narrow reads remain, including Plan 04 `:90`, Plan 09 `:125`, Plan 11 `:132`, and Plan 12 `:125`. |
| M8 | RESOLVED | Plan 01 `:302` records the phase base; Plan 16 `:148–152` compares that base with the working tree, covering committed changes too. |
| M9 | RESOLVED | `38.9-15-PLAN.md:110` explicitly preserves the edit/reset operation and changes labels only. The owner’s ruling applies; no reversal is raised. |
| M10 | RESOLVED | `38.9-14-PLAN.md:119` specifies an implementable TypeScript AST walk with literal/template/JSX coverage and explicit exceptions. Existing compiler-API precedent is `src/theme/__contract__/bare-text-sites.ts:60,522`. |
| L1 | PARTIAL | Plan 01 `:208` and Plan 02 `:127,135` deduplicate hook prompts. Plan 03’s direct `confirmDiscard()` callers (`:98,125`) do not share that protection. |
| L2 | RESOLVED | `38.9-17-PLAN.md:91` explicitly tests destructive confirmation over another registered overlay, checking that neither closes on Back. |
| L3 | RESOLVED | `38.9-11-PLAN.md:110,115` separates visual Off from accessibility `checked`, which follows the stored value. |
| L4 | DEFERRED-EXPLICITLY | D-62 at `38.9-CONTEXT.md:152` retains the chosen outline and records contrast for a later look pass; Plan 11 `:93` preserves it. |
| L5 | RESOLVED | `38.9-07-PLAN.md:163,166` adds type-level coverage of every `FabNavigateIntent["screen"]` member by `FAB_ROUTES`. |
| L6 | RESOLVED | `38.9-13-PLAN.md:148,157` adds a separate `dateKey` and tests it independently of display `tag`. |
| L7 | RESOLVED | `38.9-17-PLAN.md:92` adds closing-sheet host handoff and keyboard-open snackbar observations. |
| L8 | RESOLVED | `38.9-17-PLAN.md:88` now cites D-28 for dependency removal. Moving removal to Plan 12 was optional. |

### New concerns

- **MEDIUM — N1: “Committed” means write started, allowing lost edits and suppressed retries. Plan 03.**  
  `38.9-03-PLAN.md:155` advances `committedVersionRef` when saving starts; `:150–151` then treats that version as committed and eligible for reseeding. If a blur write fails, leaving the screen skips retry because the versions match. An outstanding refresh can also publish the old database value while the write is pending. The existing refresh directly seeds both fields (`src/screens/BackupSettingsScreen.tsx:91–100`); `commitThenRefresh` explicitly distinguishes write failure from success (`src/screens/backup-settings-logic.ts:185–202`).

  **Exact edit:** Replace “committed when save starts” with separate pending and successfully committed versions. Advance committed version only after successful persistence. Prevent reseeding from reads started before the relevant write, and preserve retry eligibility after failure. Add deferred-Promise tests for refresh-during-save, failed blur followed by Back, submit-plus-blur deduplication, and editing back to the old saved value while another value is pending.

- **LOW — N2: Pending-note close can enqueue duplicate discard prompts. Plans 03/17.**  
  Plan 03 `:98` calls `confirmDiscard()` directly from both close paths. Those paths are independently wired at `src/components/PendingConfirmationsSheet.tsx:157,163`; neither receives Plan 02’s hook-local pending ref.

  **Exact edit:** Add a synchronous pending-prompt ref to `requestSheetClose`, cleared in `finally`, and test repeated close requests before resolution. Extend Plan 17’s double-request check to this overlay.

- **MEDIUM — N3: Narrow reading instructions remain contrary to the repository rule. Plans 04/09/11/12.**  
  Plan 04 `:90`, Plan 09 `:125`, Plan 11 `:132`, and Plan 12 `:125` still direct executors to selected regions or import lines. These conversions share dirty state, navigation, and persistence behavior; reviewing only the controls can miss those connections.

  **Exact edit:** Replace these instructions with full-file reads plus the named parents, stores, and shared-state consumers. Keep cited lines as change locators, not reading boundaries.

Plan 02’s `requestClose(close, dirty)` and RelationshipEditor contracts otherwise align with Plan 10’s consumption and sequential ordering. Its size estimate alone does not establish that an owner-approved split is necessary.

The AST glossary approach is feasible and catches the cited `POPULATION_LABELS` interpolation at its literal definition. It remains a literal-source contract: it does not reconstruct arbitrary concatenation or inspect `app.config.ts`. Its guarantees should be understood within that boundary.

### Known-gap verdicts

**(a) Capture inline-name prompt lacks deduplication — real finding, LOW.**  
Plan 03 `:125` calls `confirmDiscard()` from the BackHandler without a pending ref. The handler consumes each event (`src/screens/CaptureScreen.tsx:216–222`), so repeated events before DialogHost opens can enqueue multiple prompts.

**PLAN.md edit:** Add one pending ref shared by Capture’s inline-name and note branches; consume subsequent Back events without enqueueing. Test two immediate Back events and add Capture to Plan 17’s double-Back check.

**(b) No D-61 Cancel device check — real finding, LOW.**  
Plan 17 `:89` tests header/system/gesture Back, but does not exercise editor Cancel. Plan 02’s source contracts (`:175,215`) verify wiring rather than the actual interaction.

**PLAN.md edit:** Add dirty Cancel → Keep editing/Discard, clean Cancel → immediate close, and successful save → no prompt for Update Contact—including its nested editors—Custom Fields, and Memory.

**(c) Memory Back leaves the route instead of closing its draft — real finding, MEDIUM; [POSSIBLE DECISION REVERSAL] if retained.**  
MemoryEditor has an in-screen draft and a distinct close operation (`src/components/MemoryEditor.tsx:234–249`). Plan 02 `:173` reports only dirty state and installs a route guard; it exposes draft closure only to Cancel. Consequently, clean Back leaves the screen, and confirmed dirty Back dispatches route removal rather than returning to the Memory list. That conflicts with D-14’s innermost-first rule.

**PLAN.md edit:** Give MemoryEditor optional draft-open/dirty reporting and a close-draft handle, analogous to RelationshipEditor. MemoryScreen supplies these through the guard’s `subEditor` option. Test clean and dirty Back closing the draft while retaining the route, followed by a second Back leaving. Preserve the successful-save bypass. Update Plan 17’s expected destination accordingly. Keeping route-exit behavior instead requires an **[OWNER?]** amendment to D-14.

### Risk Assessment

**MEDIUM.** The main foundations now have explicit contracts, and the owner rulings are respected. Backup persistence still has a concrete failure/race gap, while Memory’s planned Back behavior conflicts with the accepted navigation rule. Targeted edits and behavioral tests can close these issues without re-architecture or increasing plan count.

## Cycle 2 — Claude Review

### 3. New concerns

1. **MEDIUM — Plans 14 and 15: the glossary AST contract will mis-fire.** "Allowlist string literals that are route names, test IDs, log scopes" cannot be implemented as stated.
   - **False positives.** A grep finds about 173 non-test string literals containing "dashboard", mostly import specifiers, `DashboardTab`/`DashboardStack`, testIDs and property keys. A grep also finds 46 non-comment lines with "group event", including developer-facing errors such as `group-event-refresh.ts:185`. Plan 15's site list is smaller than that, so the contract will fail on unlisted sites.
   - **False negatives.** A file-level allowlist hides real UI copy.
   - **Edit to Plan 14 Task 1 and Plan 15 Task 1.** Specify the visited positions:
     - Visit only `JsxText`, JSX attribute values for `accessibilityLabel|accessibilityHint|title|label|placeholder|aria*`, and property values keyed `label|title|message|caption|heading|description|text`.
     - Also visit arguments to `showSnackbar`, `confirm` and `notice`, plus template spans inside those positions.
     - Skip import/export specifiers, property keys, `LiteralTypeNode` and `Logger.*` arguments.
     - Match retired capitalised tokens case-sensitively.
     - Match lowercase variants ("dashboard", "favourite", "group event") only when the literal contains whitespace.
     - Add self-tests for each skip rule.
   - **Decision for Plan 15.** State what happens to developer-only `Error(...)` strings. Either they are out of scope or they are listed.

2. **MEDIUM [OWNER?] — Plan 02 size.** It has 25 files in `files_modified`, three tasks, and full reads of very large files (`CreateContactScreen`, `UpdateContactScreen`, `CustomFieldsScreen`, `FieldDefForm`, `RelationshipEditor`, `AIPersonalizationScreen`). The estimate is 165k at low confidence, the highest in the phase. Treat this as a budget risk, not a split request. If the owner wants a seam, Task 3 (sub-editors and D-61) is the natural one. No split is proposed here.

3. **MEDIUM — Plan 03 Task 3: leave-flush gaps.**
   - **Tab switch.** Leaving by switching tabs fires neither `beforeRemove` nor unmount, because the Settings stack stays mounted. A typed valid count is not saved until a later leave.
     - Edit: also flush on the screen's blur (`useFocusEffect` cleanup or the navigation `blur` event), keeping `shouldFlushOnLeave` as the gate.
   - **`flushedRef`.** A `flushedRef` set in `beforeRemove` blocks the flush on a later real unmount if the removal was prevented elsewhere.
     - Edit: set it only in the unmount cleanup, or reset it on focus.
   - **Double commit.** `onSubmitEditing` followed by `onBlur` can issue two commits for one value. "Valid and unchanged" is judged against `savedValueRef`, which is stale until the refresh finishes.
     - Edit: gate blur and submit on `typedVersion !== committedVersion`, as the leave path already does.

### 4. Known-gap verdicts

- **(a) Capture inline-name prompt has no double-Back dedupe: real, LOW.** Once the dialog is open, Android hardware Back goes to the dialog's `onRequestClose` (a no-op for non-dismissable), so the Capture `BackHandler` is not reached. The window is only a fast double press before the dialog mounts.
  - The same window exists in Plan 03 Task 1's sheet close (Back plus scrim) and in Plan 05's Profile editors, which call `confirmDiscard()` outside the hook.
  - Cheapest fix: make `confirmDiscard()` single-flight in Plan 01 Task 2. While a discard prompt is pending, a second call resolves `false` (stay). Do not share the promise, or the removal action would be dispatched twice. This covers (a), Plan 03 and Plan 05 together.

- **(b) Plan 17 has no D-61 Cancel device check: real, MEDIUM-LOW.** The CONTEXT device checks are the only place the real-device behavior of the new Cancel wiring is verified. Add one bullet to Plan 17 Task 1:
  - Dirty Cancel prompts and clean Cancel closes at once, on the Update Contact field editor, including a nested custom field and the key-person form.
  - The same on the Custom Fields editor and on + → Memory.
  - A successful save in each closes with no prompt.

- **(c) + → Memory Back skips the draft: real, MEDIUM, and an unmet D-14.** `MemoryScreen` lists memories, and `MemoryEditor`'s `draft` starts `null` (`MemoryEditor.tsx:182-206, 222-249`). Opening it with Add or an existing card is a true sub-editor. The ledger lists G-12 under D-14. Plan 02 Task 2 guards only the route, so:
  - Back with a dirty draft prompts, and Discard leaves the whole screen.
  - Back with a clean draft leaves the screen without closing the draft first.
  - Cancel with a dirty draft closes only the draft. The two exits are inconsistent.
  - Edit to Plan 02 Task 2: give `MemoryEditor` the same `onDraftStateChange({ open, dirty })` and a `closeDraft` ref that Plan 02 Task 3 gives `RelationshipEditor`. Then pass `subEditor: { open, dirty, close: closeDraft }` to `MemoryScreen`'s guard. Add a case to `editor-cancel-contract.test.ts` and a Plan 17 bullet.

### 5. Risk assessment: MEDIUM

The fixer's changes hold up against the code, and the three HIGHs are closed or nearly so. No change reverses a recorded decision. The residual risk is mostly in execution:
- The glossary contracts (Plans 14 and 15) can fail the wave gate for reasons unrelated to user-visible copy.
- Plan 02 is large.
- The backup leave-flush and the Memory sub-editor each leave a user-visible gap.

All are fixable with targeted edits to the plan text.

## Cycle 3 — Codex Review

### 1. Summary

The cycle-2 fixes address six findings fully; C2-M2 and C2-M3 remain partial because the revised save controller and glossary matcher introduce concrete gaps. I found **two new MEDIUM concerns**, both fixable within the existing plans. The single-flight discard behavior is coherent: duplicate callers resolve “stay,” while the original caller owns the close or removal. Memory’s savedTick bypass is confined to successful-save navigation; its prescribed Back/Cancel paths otherwise close the draft first. This was a focused source review, not certification of every subsystem. Graph queries failed with an IPC `EPERM`; no files were edited.

### 2. Cycle-2 resolution table

Plan references below are under `.planning/phases/38.9-ux-standards/`.

| ID | Status | Evidence |
|---|---|---|
| C2-M1 | RESOLVED | `38.9-02-PLAN.md:175` wires Memory’s open/dirty/close sub-editor; `:169` specifies clean Back, dirty Discard, subsequent route exit and save bypass. |

| C2-M3 | PARTIAL | `38.9-14-PLAN.md:119` supplies AST exclusions and self-tests; `38.9-15-PLAN.md:124` defines thrown-message scope. Its punctuation boundary rule still hides real visible copy—see concern 2. |
| C2-L1 | RESOLVED | `38.9-03-PLAN.md:157` flushes from focus cleanup, covering tab switches. |
| C2-L2 | RESOLVED | Full-file/shared-state reading replaces the narrow instructions at `38.9-04-PLAN.md:90`, `38.9-09-PLAN.md:125`, `38.9-11-PLAN.md:132`, `38.9-12-PLAN.md:125`, and `38.9-13-PLAN.md:145,167`. |

### 3. New concerns

#### MEDIUM — Plan 03: leaving during a pending save can lose the latest value or enqueue duplicate writes

**Evidence:** `38.9-03-PLAN.md:150` skips text equal to `savedValue`, and blocks only `typedVersion === pendingVersion`; `:156` permits a newer write to replace the pending version; `:157` runs three leave backstops without specifying a completion-triggered drain. Existing `src/screens/backup-settings-logic.ts:178` awaits refresh before reporting a committed write, extending the pending interval.

**Mechanism:**

- Saved value is 7; version 1 starts saving 14. The user types 7 again and leaves before version 1 settles. Every leave check sees text equal to the old saved value and skips it. Version 1 then persists 14; nothing schedules the desired 7.
- If version 2 is pending when version 1 finishes its refresh, committed becomes 1 while pending remains 2. A later edit to version 3 can replace pending with 3; further lifecycle callbacks can revisit versions no longer represented by that single slot. Tracking one pending version is insufficient if overlapping writes are allowed.

The proposed fourth deferred test calls for a flush **after** settlement, but does not require the actual leave path to arrange that continuation.

**Exact recommended PLAN.md edit — Task 3:**

> Serialize writes per field. While a write is pending, retain the latest requested text/version rather than starting another write. A leave event records a drain request; after the pending write settles, re-evaluate the latest text against the successfully persisted value and drain any valid changed value, even if the screen has unmounted. Record write success immediately after `updateAppSettings` resolves, independently of refresh completion. Test: save 14 from saved 7, type 7, fire beforeRemove/focus cleanup/unmount before resolving the write, then resolve it; without invoking another test-only flush, assert the final persisted value is 7 and each required version is written once.

#### MEDIUM — Plans 14/15: treating sentence punctuation as token characters makes the glossary contract miss visible retired terms

**Evidence:** `38.9-14-PLAN.md:119` and `38.9-15-PLAN.md:123` treat `.` and `:` as word characters. Actual visible text includes `"Couldn't save this group event. Please try again."` at `src/screens/GroupLogScreen.tsx:101`, rendered through its error state at `:188`.

**Mechanism:** The period immediately after `event` prevents the required whole-word boundary. Consequently, the contract can pass with that retired phrase unchanged. The same rule misses ordinary copy such as `"Dashboard."` or `"Dashboard: …"`. Current self-tests prove token exclusions but do not prove sentence punctuation is caught.

**Exact recommended PLAN.md edit — Plan 14 Task 1, inherited by Plan 15:**

> Treat sentence punctuation as word boundaries. Exclude paths, URLs and technical tokens using their structural context or an explicit token recognizer, rather than making `.` and `:` universal word characters. Add positive self-tests for `"Couldn't save this group event. Please try again."`, `"Dashboard."`, and `"Dashboard: choose a view"`, alongside negative tests for `orbit://favourites` and module/path tokens.

### 4. Risk assessment

**MEDIUM** — The guard fixes are coherent, but backup saves need an explicit pending-write drain, and glossary tests currently miss ordinary sentence-ending terms.

## Cycle 3 — Claude Review

> Lane note: this lane states it did not re-open source files; its findings rest on the plan text and are weighted below a source-grounded review. The orchestrator re-checked every finding counted below on disk.

### 1. Summary

All eight cycle-2 findings are resolved in the plan text, with 0 HIGH and no new owner-level contradictions. I did not re-open source files in this pass. Every file:line claim below comes from the plan text or the CONTEXT facts, and the points that depend on disk contents are marked (unverified). The single-flight `confirmDiscard()` is consistent with the hook's `promptPendingRef` and with the direct callers in Plans 03 and 05. The residual issues are:
- a stuck-flag failure mode in the module-level single-flight;
- hook mocks that will crash after the hook's return type changes;
- repeated retries on a failed backup save;
- two holes in Plan 14/15's glossary contract.

**Required interactions, checked.**
- **Direct callers:** Capture's BackHandler always returns true, so a second Back while the prompt is pending is consumed. The pending-sheet close resolves false, so `onClose` runs at most once. The Profile editors keep their Sheet open. None of these loses an action or removes anything twice.
- **Plan 02 MemoryEditor wiring:**
  - Clean Back closes the draft and stays.
  - Dirty Back prompts, and Discard closes the draft and stays.
  - A save closes through `commit()`'s direct `close()`, and `savedTick` sets the bypass before `goBack()`, so no prompt fires.
  - The "Move to Recently Deleted" path closes the draft without leaving, which is intended.
  - I found no way for dirty data to leave unprompted, and no way for a clean exit to be prompted, provided the hook reads the latest `subEditor`. See N4.

### 3. New concerns

**N1 — LOW/MEDIUM — Plan 03 Task 3: a failed save is retried and announced three times.**
- Mechanism: a failed leave-write clears `pendingVersionRef` and leaves `committedVersionRef` behind. Each of the three flush sites (focus cleanup, `beforeRemove`, unmount) then passes `shouldFlushOnLeave` again. A single Back therefore retries and shows the error snackbar up to three times.
- Edit: add a per-leave "attempted this leave" guard. A flush that already attempted version V in this leave does not run again until the next keystroke or the screen regains focus. Add a test: one failed write, then focus-cleanup, `beforeRemove` and unmount, gives exactly one attempt and one snackbar.

**N2 — LOW — Plan 03 Task 3: the controller is optional, but the acceptance tests depend on it.**
- Mechanism: "executor's choice" on extracting a pure controller means the four deferred-promise tests cannot run without rendering the screen. An executor who skips the extraction either gets vacuous tests or none.
- Edit: make the controller mandatory. Say "the version state lives in `createBackupDayController` in `backup-settings-logic.ts`, and the screen only forwards events."

**N3 — MEDIUM — Plan 01 Task 2: a single-flight flag that never clears blocks every later discard.**
- Mechanism: the module-level pending flag is cleared "in `finally` when that prompt resolves". If `DialogHost` is unmounted, the store is reset, or a Fast Refresh remount drops the queue entry, the promise never settles. The flag stays set and every later `confirmDiscard()` resolves false, so Back on a dirty form does nothing for the rest of the session.
- Edit:
  - Have `confirm-store` reject or resolve false for dropped entries when the store is reset.
  - Clear the flag in a `resetConfirmStore()` that tests call.
  - Add a test: a pending prompt, then the store reset, then the next `confirmDiscard()` prompts again.

**N4 — MEDIUM — Plans 02 and 10: mocked `useDiscardKeepGuard` returns `undefined`, so `const { requestClose } = …` throws.**
- Evidence (unverified): Plan 10 reads `SystemBuilderScreen.test.tsx`'s guard mock at ~`:101`. Plan 10 Task 2's new `custom-fields-header-render.test.tsx` renders CustomFieldsScreen, which now destructures `requestClose`.
- Edit: add to Plan 02 Task 1: "every test that mocks `useDiscardKeepGuard` must return `{ requestClose: vi.fn() }`. Grep the mocks, including `MemoryScreen`, `UpdateContact` and `CustomFields` tests, and update them in the same commit." Add the same line to Plan 10 Task 2's render-test instructions. Also state that the hook's `beforeRemove` listener reads `hasUnsavedChanges` and `subEditor` through refs, or that the effect deps include them. Otherwise the listener sees a stale `subEditor.open`, because `onDraftStateChange` fires from an effect.

**N5 — MEDIUM — Plans 14 and 15: the "Group Event" contract misses the plural.**
- Mechanism: the capitalised whole-word match for "Group Event" does not match "Group Events". `dashboard-overflow-actions.ts:28` ("Group Events", converted to `EVENTS` in Plan 15 Task 2) is the example. A regression back to "Group Events" would pass.
- Edit: the contract regexes should be `Group Events?` and `group events?`, and `Favourites?`, `Dashboards?` likewise. Add a self-test for the plural.

**N6 — LOW/MEDIUM — Plan 14: skip rule (e) and the file scope.**
- Mechanism:
  - Skipping every `Error(…)` argument app-wide hides retired terms in any thrown message that a screen later shows via `catch (e) { showSnackbar({ label: e.message }) }`. Plan 15 verified this only for `group-event-refresh.ts` and the `merge-dao.ts` `super(…)`.
  - The contract walks `src/**` only, so `app.config.ts` (the widget label "Orbit — Favourites", Plan 14 Task 2) is unguarded.
- Edit:
  - Before applying (e), run a one-time grep for `catch … \.message` flowing into `showSnackbar` or `notice`. If any exist, drop (e) and use an exact-text allowlist instead.
  - Add `app.config.ts` to the walk's file set.
- Also LOW: lowercase single-word user text, or concatenation such as `"Reset " + "dashboard"`, is missed by the whitespace rule. State this as an accepted gap.

**N7 — LOW — D-63 `[OWNER?]`.**
- Mechanism: CONTEXT D-63 labels the ruling `[DECIDED]` but derives it from D-41, while the ledger calls it an orchestrator enforcement. Renaming "Reset Overrides" and the "Overrides" badge is a label change to a surface that milestone-2 D-09-088 locks. If D-09-088's text names those labels, this is a partial reversal and not a D-41 enforcement.
- Edit: check D-09-088's wording before Plan 15 executes. If it names the labels, mark the entry as an owner ruling in the ledger. Separately, add D-61–D-63 to the ledger's decision index; it stops at D-49.

## Cycle 3 — Actionable Non-HIGH Concerns (history)

### MEDIUM

- **C3-M1 — Backup: leaving while a day-field write is pending can persist the wrong value, and test (4) contradicts the mechanism.** Codex; claude N2 folded in; **verified**. Plan **03**, Task 3.
  - Evidence: `shouldFlushOnLeave` returns false when the text equals `savedValue` (`38.9-03-PLAN.md:150`). `savedValueRef` advances only on success (`:155`), and the leave-flush runs at leave time (`:157`).
  - Failure: saved is 7. A blur write of 14 is pending. The user types 7 and leaves. Every leave check sees "7 == saved 7" and skips, then the write of 14 lands, so the DB holds 14 while the field showed 7.
  - Test (4) at `:152` expects "a leave-flush writes the latest text once the pending write settles", but nothing in the action arranges that. An executor either cannot pass the test or writes it vacuously, for example by calling the flush by hand after settling.
  - PLAN.md edit, Plan 03 Task 3:
    - Compare the latest text against the pending value when a write is pending, and against `savedValueRef` otherwise.
    - When a leave event fires while a field's write is pending, record a drain request. When that write settles, success or failure, re-evaluate `shouldFlushOnLeave` on the latest text and write it if needed. The drain is a ref-held closure, so it runs even after unmount.
    - Make the pure `createBackupDayController` in `backup-settings-logic.ts` mandatory: it holds the version state and drain logic, and the screen only forwards events. Drop "executor's choice; this plan only".
    - Rewrite test (4): saved 7; start the 14 write; type 7; fire focus-cleanup, `beforeRemove` and unmount; resolve the write. Then assert, with no test-only flush call, that the final persisted value is 7 and each version was written once.

- **C3-M2 — The glossary matcher misses sentence-final, plural and sentence-case retired terms, so the contract passes vacuously for them.** Codex; claude N5 merged; **verified** by grep. Plans **14** (Task 1) and **15** (Task 1).
  - Evidence: `38.9-14-PLAN.md:119` and `38.9-15-PLAN.md:123` treat `.` and `:` as word characters and match whole words case-sensitively.
  - Real visible copy the contract would therefore miss:
    - `GroupLogScreen.tsx:101` "…this group event. Please…"
    - `EditGroupEventScreen.tsx:155,214,248` and `GroupEventDetailScreen.tsx:206,261` ("…group event.")
    - `EditParticipantScreen.tsx:54` and `HistorySection.tsx:1290`
    - `HomeScreen.tsx:1426` "…favourite. Try again." and `HomeScreen.tsx:2255` "…the Dashboard. You…"
    - `GroupLogScreen.tsx:26` "Group events can't…": sentence case, which neither the case-sensitive "Group Event" nor "group event" matches
    - `dashboard-overflow-actions.ts:28` "Group Events": plural
  - Plans 14 and 15 do list these sites for conversion, so the effect is that the contract cannot catch a missed or regressed site, not that a site goes unconverted.
  - PLAN.md edit, Plan 14 Task 1 (Plan 15 Task 1 inherits it):
    - Treat sentence punctuation (`.` `:` `,` `!` `?`) as word boundaries. Only `_ - /` and an adjacent letter or digit join a token. Rely on skip rules (a)–(e) plus a URL/scheme check (`://`) to keep paths and schemes out.
    - Match the plural optionally (`Group Events?`, `group events?`, `Dashboards?`).
    - Make the multi-word lowercase forms match the sentence-case variant too (`[Gg]roup events?`, `[Nn]ot yet contacted`).
    - Add positive self-tests for "Couldn't save this group event. Please try again.", "Dashboard." / "Dashboard: x", "Group events can't…" and "Group Events". Add negative self-tests for `orbit://favourites`, `favourite_rank` and `./dashboard-read`.
    - Add `app.config.ts` to the walked file set, so the widget label that Plan 14 Task 2 converts is guarded.

### LOW

- **C3-L1 — A failed leave-flush can retry and show the snackbar up to three times on one exit.** Claude N1; **verified** by reasoning against `38.9-03-PLAN.md:155-157`. Plan **03**, Task 3.
  - After a failure, pending clears and committed stays behind, so each of the three leave sites (focus cleanup, `beforeRemove`, unmount) can pass the gate again once the earlier attempt has settled.
  - PLAN.md edit: record `leaveAttemptedVersion` per field. A leave site skips a version that has already been attempted during this leave. The record resets on the next keystroke or on focus. Add a test: one failing write, then all three leave events, gives exactly one attempt and one snackbar.

- **C3-L2 — The single-flight flag is module state that tests can strand.** Claude N3, downgraded from MEDIUM; **verified** against `38.9-01-PLAN.md:209`.
  - In production every queued entry settles: DialogHost is always mounted, and destructive entries settle only through resolveCurrent. Fast Refresh is dev-only.
  - In tests, a case that leaves a prompt unresolved makes every later `confirmDiscard()` in that file resolve false, which is a confusing failure for the executor.
  - PLAN.md edit, Plan 01 Task 2: export a test-only `resetConfirmStore()` that clears the queue and the pending flag (or derive "pending" from the queue holding a discard entry instead of a separate flag). Call it in `beforeEach` in `confirm-store.test.ts` and `discard-keep-guard.test.ts`.

### New concerns

### C4-M1 — MEDIUM — Plan 03, Task 3: an old controller can overwrite a newer screen’s save

**Evidence:** `38.9-03-PLAN.md:156` creates a controller per mount while allowing its drain to continue after unmount; `:157-158` gives each controller independent version state. The current screen reads settings on focus at `src/screens/BackupSettingsScreen.tsx:91-112`. Writes use the shared transaction at `src/db/app-settings-dao.ts:1422-1433`, which serializes through `src/db/transaction.ts:52-61` and `src/db/mutex.ts:32-35`.

**Mechanism:** Mount A starts writing 14, then the user types 7 and leaves. Before A settles, mount B opens and submits 21. The mutex can queue writes as **14 → 21 → A’s deferred 7**: A enqueues its drain only after 14 settles. The final persisted value becomes 7, overwriting the newer edit. Per-controller version numbers cannot detect this.

**Exact recommended PLAN.md edit:** Add to Task 3’s controller contract:

> Day-field write authority and pending/drain state must survive screen remounts. A new screen attaches to that authority rather than creating an independent writer; its newer edits supersede an older mount’s deferred drain. Keep render subscriptions mount-local and retain save-after-unmount behavior.

Add a deferred test:

> Start 14 on mount A, type 7, leave and detach A; attach mount B and submit 21 before 14 settles. Settle all writes without a test-only flush. Assert the final persisted value is 21 and A cannot subsequently overwrite it.

### C4-M2 — MEDIUM — Plan 02, Task 3: Frequency’s dirty check misses invalid typed text

**Evidence:** `38.9-02-PLAN.md:213` specifies “Frequency vs header seed.” The parent tracks `intervalDays` and validity at `src/screens/UpdateContactScreen.tsx:623-624,667-671`. Raw text lives inside `src/components/FrequencyPicker.tsx:69`; `:95-100` calls the parent’s `onChange` only when parsing succeeds.

**Mechanism:** Type an invalid custom interval such as `0`. The visible text changes, but `intervalDays` remains equal to its seed. The prescribed comparator reports clean, so Back or Cancel drops typed text without the D-14/D-61 prompt.

**Exact recommended PLAN.md edit:** Replace “Frequency vs header seed” with:

> Frequency compares its raw custom-input draft and selected unit against the editor’s seed, as well as the emitted interval. Add an optional draft-state callback to FrequencyPicker so invalid typed text reaches the guard; opening an untouched Custom control alone is not dirty.

Add FrequencyPicker and its relevant tests to Task 3’s file/read lists. Add a behavior test: invalid typing produces no interval `onChange`, but Back prompts and Keep editing preserves the raw text.

### C4-M3 — MEDIUM — Plan 02, Task 3: Off Limits uses the wrong edit baseline

**Evidence:** `38.9-02-PLAN.md:213` specifies “OffLimits draft vs `""`,” despite `:205` requiring comparison against the editor’s seed. Editing an existing item sets its stored text into the draft at `src/screens/UpdateContactScreen.tsx:476-478`; the input edits that draft at `:496-499`.

**Mechanism:** An untouched existing item appears dirty because its text is nonempty. Conversely, clearing that existing text to empty appears clean, allowing Back/Cancel to discard the change without prompting.

**Exact recommended PLAN.md edit:** Replace “OffLimits draft vs `""`” with:

> OffLimits compares against a per-edit seed: empty for a new item, the item’s text when an existing item is selected. Replace the seed when the selected item changes.

Add cases: untouched existing item is clean; clearing or changing it is dirty; restoring its original text is clean.

### C4-M4 — MEDIUM — Plan 02, Task 2: section refresh silently discards Writing Style edits

**Evidence:** `38.9-02-PLAN.md:176` sets `savedStyleRef` in every `load()`. That function unconditionally replaces the current style at `src/screens/AIPersonalizationScreen.tsx:184-192`. Adding, importing, editing, reordering and deleting sections call it at `:289,321,337,388,401`. Writing Style is a four-field object, not a string: `src/db/personalization-dao.ts:11-16`; the plan’s `:170` specifies an exact string comparison.

**Mechanism:** Edit Writing Style, then reorder or save a personalization section. `load()` replaces the unsaved style with the database value and the proposed implementation re-baselines it. The text disappears without a discard prompt, and the guard subsequently reports clean.

**Exact recommended PLAN.md edit:** Replace the Writing Style action with:

> Compare a stable signature of tone, length, directness and exact freeform text. Seed style and its baseline on initialization; section-only refreshes must not replace or re-baseline an edited style. After save, re-baseline the exact submitted snapshot, preserving any edits made while that write was pending.

Add tests for unsaved style followed by section refresh, and for another edit during a deferred save.

### C4-M5 — MEDIUM — Plan 08, Task 3: the pending-header contract fails before conversion

**Evidence:** `38.9-08-PLAN.md:142` puts header-less screens in `PENDING_HEADER_CONVERSION` and requires pending screens to retain their “current Back.” Plan 09 explicitly identifies no existing Back for SurvivorSelect and MergeImpactSummary at `38.9-09-PLAN.md:95-97`. Their current render branches contain no Back: `src/screens/SurvivorSelectScreen.tsx:81-128` and `src/components/MergeImpactSummary.tsx:111-150`.

**Mechanism:** Applying the prescribed Back assertion to the pending set makes Plan 08’s gate fail on screens that legitimately have no Back until Plans 09–10 add their headers.

**Exact recommended PLAN.md edit:** Replace the pending-set assertion with:

> Record each pending screen’s current chrome category: existing in-body Back or currently header-less. Assert retention of in-body Back only for the former; assert the expected header-less state for the latter. Remove either entry in its converting commit.

Add detector cases for one pending screen in each category.

### C4-L1 — LOW — Plan 16, Task 1: full-file reading requirement remains incomplete

**Evidence:** `38.9-16-PLAN.md:95-96` requests the private helpers/call sites and HomeScreen “at the A-16 lines.”

**Exact recommended PLAN.md edit:** Replace those bullets with:

> Read each touched file in full, plus its shared-state consumers and asserting tests, per CLAUDE.md “Review the code, not the diff”; the A-16 lines locate the changes.

This is the remaining M7 instruction gap.

### C4-L2 — LOW — All plans: an execution constraint has no supplied source

**Evidence:** Every plan’s execution notes state “No AI attribution in commits” under a CLAUDE.md heading—for example `38.9-01-PLAN.md:297` and `38.9-16-PLAN.md:188`. CLAUDE.md contains no such rule; its source requirement is at `CLAUDE.md:98-100`.

**Exact recommended PLAN.md edit:** Either cite the actual owner note containing this instruction, or change it to:

> No AI attribution in commits — this plan only.

Do not present it as a CLAUDE.md standing rule without provenance.

### Risk assessment

**MEDIUM** — The identified gaps can silently discard drafts, overwrite a newer backup setting, or block a wave’s required gate; incomplete source coverage prevents broader clearance.

### New concerns

### N1 — MEDIUM — Plan 03 Task 3: a drain started by blur sets `leaveAttemptedVersion`, so a failed value is never retried on leave

**Evidence.** `38.9-03-PLAN.md:158`:

- "Blur or submit while a write is pending … records a drain request".
- The drain "run[s] the same leave check … sets `leaveAttemptedVersion = typedVersion`".
- A leave site "skips the field when `typedVersion === leaveAttemptedVersion`".
- Only a keystroke or the screen regaining focus resets it.

**Failure.**

1. Saved value is 7. The user types 14 and blurs, so the write of 14 starts.
2. The user types 21 and blurs while that write is still pending, so a drain is recorded.
3. The write of 14 succeeds. The drain writes 21 and marks v(21) as attempted.
4. The write of 21 fails, and the snackbar shows.
5. The user presses Back with no further keystroke. The leave check finds v(21) already attempted and skips it.

The database keeps 14 while the field showed 21. This contradicts must-have `:32`, "a failed save is retried when the screen is left". None of tests (1)–(5) covers it.

**Plan edit (`38.9-03-PLAN.md:157-158` and `:152`).**

- Add a per-field `drainOrigin: "blur" | "leave" | null`. A blur/submit sets it to "blur" unless it is already "leave"; any leave site sets it to "leave".
- When the drain runs, set `leaveAttemptedVersion` only if `drainOrigin === "leave"`. A blur-origin drain behaves like a blur commit and leaves `leaveAttemptedVersion` untouched.
- Add test (6): saved 7; blur starts the write of 14; type 21 and blur (pending); resolve 14 as success; reject the drained 21; then fire `beforeRemove` → exactly one more write of 21.
- Add the `drainOrigin` field to the field-state list at `:157`.

### N2 — LOW — Plans 14 and 15 Task 1: the Error and Logger skip rules do not say they cover template pieces nested inside an argument

**Evidence.** `14-PLAN:119` rules (d) and (e) say "argument to". But `group-events-dao.ts:256,698` pass `` `Group Event id=${…}` `` to `new Error(…)`. The text nodes there are `TemplateHead` / `TemplateTail`, whose parent is a `TemplateExpression`, not the call.

**Failure.** An executor who checks only `node.parent` gets false positives. The contract fails loudly, so this is not a silent pass, but it costs an executor cycle.

**Plan edit.** In rules (d) and (e), write "any literal or template piece whose nearest enclosing call argument belongs to a `Logger.*` / `Error(…)` call". Add a self-test with a template argument: `` new Error(`Group Event id=${x}`) `` is skipped.

### N3 — LOW — Unsourced constants (CLAUDE.md "Rules carry their source")

- Plan 13 `:172`: the 40-character truncation for the search echo. D-20 says only "truncated".
- Plan 11 `:109`, `:115`: disabled `opacity 0.5`. D-26 says only "dimmed".

**Plan edit.** Label each one "this plan only", or cite its precedent, for example `Button.tsx`'s disabled opacity for the 0.5.

### N4 — LOW — Plan 01 Task 3: a check that cannot do what it says

**Evidence.** `01-PLAN:240` asks the contract to assert "no file under `src/db/migrations` changed". A source-scan test cannot see changes, so this assertion would pass vacuously. It is also the check target of the D-12 prohibition at `:102-106`.

**Plan edit.** Mark the D-12 prohibition `verification: judgment` and drop that clause from the test.

- **Settings-stack navigation targets are fine.** Hosting the + forms in the Settings stack does not navigate to an unregistered route: GroupLog, UpdateContact and Memory push nothing new, and UpdateContact only navigates to Memory (`UpdateContactScreen.tsx:860`).

### Risk assessment

**LOW–MEDIUM.** The plans match the code on disk. The one MEDIUM is a narrow lost-setting path in the Backup day fields, and it is a one-clause fix in Plan 03. The rest are LOW precision edits. There are no HIGH findings and no `[OWNER?]` items.

### Dropped, downgraded or corrected
- **Codex C4-M1 (an old controller can overwrite a remounted screen's save):** downgraded to LOW (C4-L1). The mechanism is real. Each mount has its own controller (`38.9-03-PLAN.md:156`), and writes serialize through the shared mutex. But it needs the first write to stay queued while the user leaves, re-enters the Backup screen, types and blurs. The write is a local single-key SQLite update, so in practice that window is milliseconds. The fix is cheap, so it stays as LOW.
- **Codex C4-M4 (Writing Style):** kept as MEDIUM, re-scoped after verification.
  - The core defect is in the comparator spec. `38.9-02-PLAN.md:170` says "exact string compare", but `WritingStyle` is a four-field object (`src/db/personalization-dao.ts:11-16`). An executor following it compares only `freeform`, so tone, length and directness edits never prompt.
  - The section-refresh clobber is real: `load()` at `AIPersonalizationScreen.tsx:184-192` runs from `:289,321,337,388,401`. But it already happens today. It is included only because Plan 02 `:176` re-baselines inside `load()`, which would make the guard treat lost edits as clean.
- **Claude N3 (unsourced constants):** merged into C4-L3. The 0.5 disabled opacity has a precedent at `src/components/ui/Button.tsx:92`, so it only needs a citation.
- **Claude, "Plan 08's pending list is complete" (41 header-less screens):** this corroborates C4-M5. Header-less screens are in `PENDING_HEADER_CONVERSION`, so Plan 08's "must still have their current Back" assertion fails for them.
